Utopia","volume":"婚后外传：The Donkey Medal","anchor":"chapter-0179","paragraphs":["The forest path ended at a room with no walls. Benches circled a stone hearth beneath the trees, and an old piano stood under a roof. Annel called it her western salon. Its yearly concert included a symphony reduced for two hands, which Cassian thought an ambitious arrangement. Mara was more interested in the locked box on its shelf. The room's quiet would illuminate a happy memory for Mara later.","A card told them to recite a past-tense sentence from the textbook, emphasizing the word that had changed. The answer depended on the spelling of an irregular verb. Mara found it successfully before Simon arrived. She was skillful at that sort of puzzle; he was skillful at pretending he had not been beaten. Bea asked whether they could offer up a second answer if the first failed. Annel's assistant nodded. Nothing here required immediate perfection.","The box held another prehistoric animal. Its stem-like tail pointed at a folded sheet of stationery. The clue would steer them to a room named after a famous work of fiction. Mara chose Utopia, which the inn had used as a suite name. Simon chose the television lounge because the TV show had filmed its opening there. His daughter glanced toward the printed word, then followed him with a sigh.","Cassian's phone remained mute. The forested ridge blocked the telecom signal, and the hunt's web-based page was still unavailable. He was done with checking it when Mara took it from him. \"Your wife is here. The world will cope.\"","A flower stem lay beside a little bowl of stones. The owner used the outdoor room for spiritual gatherings as well as concerts; no particular belief was required to enjoy its quiet. The sign did not warrant disturbing the objects. Simon noticed the instruction and gently moved Bea's hand away, then explained why.","\"A reasoned proposition.\"","He slipped the phone into his bag. She rewarded him with a kiss that made the paper crane on his map crumple. They could read a damaged map. She had no intention of wasting this quiet interval for the sake of a screen.","The Utopia suite displayed the same magazine article about the US Supreme Court. This copy had a word underlined in pencil. The earlier copy's illustration had supplied a direction; this one asked a question about the title. A past-tense verb had replaced a present-tense one. The change mattered because the next clue concerned a former tenant, not the person living there now. The test was reading, not courtroom trivia.","Mara looked for the tenant's letter. It lay beside a box of canceled warranties, including the old handset's warranty. The letters described the inn's yearly struggle to keep the bookshop open. Its owner had been tempted to underfund the reading room whenever the hotel kitchen needed repairs. She had eventually placed a fixed share of room income beyond that easy temptation. Well-heeled guests supported the scheme with books as well as money.","The little history made the contest feel less frivolous. Its meaningful trick was to let people roam through the place they were helping preserve. Annel's budget remained difficult, but the game offered more than a guilty appeal. Mara found a second map, against the background of a drawing of the older woods. Its shifting baseline was again the landscape people had mistaken for permanent.","Simon reached the suite late, his shoes muddy. Bea had persuaded him to read the clue again. \"We ended up behind the TV,\" she said. Her father winced.","\"A common destination,\" Cassian answered.","The joke allowed Simon to laugh at himself. A daunting task became easier when nobody needed to hide the first mistake. He helped Bea compare the forest pictures, then let her populate their answer sheet. The question they had left unanswered suddenly had a clear response. Bea tapped the earliest date with a triumphant finger.","A loose tile clattered behind the piano. The assistant stopped the teams from entering that corner and called the innkeeper. Inspectional Services Department paperwork did not make a building immune to new damage. Annel checked the roof from the safe path and closed that section until a roofer could examine it. The next clue would be moved. Nobody wanted a wooden medal enough to stand beneath a shifting slate.","Mara helped carry the piano stool away. Annel looked distressed at the disruption, but the impromptu change suited Cassian. \"An excellent opportunity to rush to help my wife,\" he said, lifting the heavy end before she could argue.","\"You were already doing that.\"","\"I wanted it appreciated.\"","She kissed his cheek in payment. Simon, who had been treating every delay as an insult, began to carry a bench. Bea watched as if her father had just performed a surprisingly difficult trick.","The replacement clue included the cherry-tree-chopping tale. Its old moral was honest admission, not proof that every detail of a fable had happened. Annel asked contestants to distinguish the narrative from an actual record. Simon admitted that he had assumed the story was historical because he had heard it so often. Mara did not tease him. A false familiarity could catch anyone.","At the stream crossing, the stepping stones were slippery. Cassian waited while Mara chose a dry line, then followed. The low water shone around her boots. She turned and held out her hand with theatrical impatience. \"Are you coming, Your Grace?\"","\"Successfully, I hope.\"","His final step splashed her. Paper","volume":"婚后外传：The Donkey Medal","anchor":"chapter-0180","paragraphs":["The final station stood beneath a prehistoric-looking pine. Its branches spread above a table covered in paper crowns, and its bark smelled warm after the rain. Mara wanted to offer up a formal complaint about the length of the route. Cassian reminded her that the treasure had always been a donkey medal. She said that was precisely why the stakes were so high.","Annel waited with the last box. \"One more past-tense question,\" she said.","Mara had expected to end up in the Utopia suite. Annel handed her its key, wrapped in a little map of the inn. Cassian thanked the owner, then asked whether the room included a place of honor for a wooden medal. Its smiling brand of donkey, happily free from the inn's usual promotion, looked ridiculous enough to delight her.","Simon groaned. Bea elbowed him before Mara could. He looked at the girl, then at the box, and smiled. \"Read it to me.\"","The clue quoted a fictional visitor to the inn. He had been obsessed with winning, then returned years later to say he remembered only the person beside him. Contestants had to recite the sentence explaining the change. No one could answer by rushing to find a hidden number. They had to read the whole short letter.","Mara took her time. The sentence was more sentimental than she would have admitted liking, yet she glanced at Cassian before writing. He knew exactly which moment she meant. His hand moved to the small of her back, and the playful competition briefly receded.","\"You cheated,\" she whispered.","\"By existing attractively?\"","\"An old habit.\"","The code opened the box, revealing a smaller envelope addressed to the inn's first owner. The final page continued the story of the visitor. He had once tried to buy the inn so he could control every room associated with a happy memory. Its owner refused to sell. Instead, she offered him the room for a week and asked him to stop treating affection as a property right. Mara laughed aloud at that.","Annel's grandfather had written the fiction for an earlier hunt. It was not a concealed deed or a legal ruling. The bookshop had made the invented letter clearly distinct from the real family papers. In the margin, a drawing of a well-heeled traveler carried a suitcase much larger than his head. Cassian studied it with an expression of professional concern.","\"Resemblance?\" Mara asked.","\"Purely accidental.\"","A letter delivered by mail invited visitors to place a bid for an illustrated edition. The small auction would occur after the melody ended. Mara left it to the collectors and asked Cassian to dance. There was no deception in his refusal: he wanted her warm feet against his before she became ambitious again.","Their last answer earned the winning stamp. Mara threw both arms around him with such force that the paper crowns scattered. Bea shrieked with delight, and Simon caught a crown before it reached the stream. He placed it on his daughter's head. Their team had finished second, an outcome he seemed finally capable of enjoying.","The donation total had risen beyond Annel's expectation. She would not have to underfund the bookshop's repairs to pay for the concert. A visitor who had once argued before the US Supreme Court had sent a check and a note saying she regretted missing the game. The letter was not a clue. After a day of decoding every object, Mara needed that reassurance.","She accepted the wooden medal as if it were a royal decoration. Cassian bowed low enough to make her laugh, then tied its ribbon behind her neck. The donkey rested against her plain shirt. It was the cheapest prize she had ever worked so hard to win, and she could not remember liking a new possession more.","Simon thanked her for singing with Bea. \"I thought I had to teach her to compete,\" he said.","\"You did. She also taught you how to finish.\"","He accepted the gentle answer. Bea asked whether he would join her for the next contest. He said yes without consulting the prize list. Mara considered that a more impressive victory than their stamp card.","The concert began at dusk. The pianist had moved to the safe covered area, where lanterns made a bright island around the instrument. Mara sat beside Cassian with her feet tucked beneath his coat. He had brought tea, and the cup warmed her chilled hands. For once, she had no objection to being extravagantly looked after.","Annel came by with the smaller prizes. Each child received a prehistoric animal model; each adult received a voucher for a return visit. The old visitors' book lay open on a nearby table. People who were skillful at games had written ordinary, unpolished thanks. The pages pleased Mara more than a line of perfect scores.","\"Would you like another treasure hunt?\" Cassian asked.","\"Eventually. Tonight I'm going to sit successfully still.\"","\"A formidable ambition.\"","He offered up the last biscuit, which she accepted without ceremony. The music floated beneath the trees. Mara reached for his hand and traced the crooked star he had drawn on her wrist. It had faded, but enough remained for her to follow its shape.","When the first tune ended, Cassian kissed her bare fingertips. Somewhere behind them, a child began to recite the donkey's nonsense song. Mara leaned against her husband and let the second tune begin."],"questions":[],"wordCount":904,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0180\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div Mara had been told it was a gentle weekend excursion. Cassian looked at the looming mountains, then at the bone-showing knees of the master of ceremonies, whose ridiculous shorts provided no defense against winter. \"An understatement,\" he said. The morning was cold without being dismal.","The announcer, Arlo Cardas, was officially out to beat the younger teams. He listed them alphabetically, gave each a co-captain, and ordered everyone to memorize the route. His paternal tone suggested that he considered the guests as children in his own feudal household. His wife, Celia, smacked him with a glove. \"You promised decent manners.\"","\"As we all know, promises must be interpreted creatively.\"","\"Only by the ruthless.\"","Linn's day-job editor had sent a forthright instruction: no cheap thrills at the expense of frightened guests. The article was aimed at the families visiting the lodge. The journalist's own thesis concerned population decline in mountain towns, but today her theme was recreation. She arranged her notes in chronological order. An old photograph of Celia's coronavirus assignment was excluded; the album would not be exclusively about disasters.","Mara liked her instantly. Celia was a well-known photographer whose work had traveled all over the world. An old picture of the White House occupied the cover of the lodge's commemorative album. Its presidential commission had once made her famous, but she valued a photograph of her late brother more. She still needed quiet days to grieve. Today she had chosen to race, with no obligation to smile for anyone's video.","The event celebrated Arlo's anti-patent campaign. He wanted small inventions shared freely and treated every gadget as a victory for liberal education. His insatiable curiosity could be exhausting, yet Mara admired the autonomous sled he had built for moving bags. Its cumulative testing records encompassed several winters. A glowing review might call it hi-tech; Celia's diary called it \"the thing that ate my mittens.\"","Linn, a young journalist, wanted to capitalize on the event's earned media. She had praised the couple glowingly in a travel feature and now hoped to illustrate the race with drone shots. Cassian asked her to keep the camera away from the first narrow turn. She answered forthrightly that she understood. A short-term film could not be worth obstructing the path. Soup at the hut was meant to counteract the chill. Arlo exclaimed at its price.","The target was a small hut halfway up the ridge. Its shaft of chimney smoke marked the turning point. A universal map was posted beside the door, but local conditions could change. Snow had fallen for three successive nights. The guide warned that inclement weather might affect the final climb. They would reassess at the checkpoint and turn back if needed. The mountains did not become safe because a sponsor liked the photograph.","Arlo could not foresee everything, he admitted, then added a boast about his engineering. Celia called him a case in point for the difference between invention and judgment. His pride was easily aggravated. Mara hoped his enthusiasm would not undermine the guide, who was trying to summarize a sensible plan. The race would encompass a short marked course, not an expedition.","Arlo liked to characterize speed as intrinsically admirable. The guide refused to let that impulse impel the group into a bad choice. His stress on fair play included obeying the route. A wrong turn could amount to hours of trouble. They could be affected by conditions unseen from the lodge, and might be confronted with ice under fresh snow. Arlo was to blame for delaying the briefing, and Celia told him so meaningfully.","The teams committed to the plan. Cassian promised to do his utmost to keep pace with his wife. Mara accepted the offer with an insatiable grin. The guide made them consider the route as a whole before choosing a pace. A quick start could devalue the energy needed later. Celia's warning was apt: a cumulative fatigue was harder to notice than one big stumble.","Arlo handed them a language quiz for the hut. Its vocabulary included a surname, a fable, and the terminology of skis. The questions would be arranged chronologically, not alphabetically, despite his fondness for sorting. \"Memorize the first answer,\" he said, \"and so forth.\"","\"That isn't a method,\" Mara told him.","\"It's a mystery.\"","The game had an assimilative spirit: newcomers were folded into the lodge's old rituals within minutes. They did not have to surrender their own habits to join. Celia asked each guest how to pronounce a name rather than conventionally shortening it. Linn wrote them down. A human rights photographer might be expected to preach dignity; Celia made it practical, one introduction at a time.","The starting horn blew. Mara pushed off recklessly, then laughed when Cassian caught up. He was expected to lose, Arlo declared, because marriage had softened him. His wife's reply was paternal only in its severity. \"Don't betray a guest's trust by insulting his wife.\"","\"He insulted me,\" Cassian pointed out.","\"A distinction my eternal affection may overlook.\"","Mara laughed so hard she nearly fell. Her face glowed, her beauty far from skin-deep to the man watching her. He touched her sleeve and steered her clear of a gaping rut. Linn instantly turned the camera toward the scenery. She had noticed the exchange without trying to own it.","At the first bend, Arlo aggressively accelerated. Celia called after him, but he had already gone. A small red marker denoted the approved trail. He passed it on the wrong side. His sled's autonomous carrier obediently followed his earlier route setting, not the guide's new one.","\"That's not the path,\" Mara said.","Cassian was moving before she finished. The competition had lasted six minutes. The rescue would require rather more than applause."],"questions":[],"wordCount":976,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0181\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：The Winter Baron</div><h1>Six Minutes of Racing</h1><p id=\"chapter-0181-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7683\" data-words=\"7683\">snow</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6697\" data-words=\"6697\">race</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-896\" data-words=\"896\">began</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9260\" data-words=\"9260\">with</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7317\" data-words=\"7317\">scream</a>, <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1090\" data-words=\"1090\">broken</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1223\" data-words=\"1223\">camera</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8005\" data-words=\"8005\">strap</a>, <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-383\" data-words=\"383\">and</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7070\" data-words=\"7070,7069\">retired</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4530\" data-words=\"4530\">inventor</a> demanding <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5338\" data-words=\"5338\">more</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5334\" data-words=\"5334\">moola</a>. Edge","volume":"婚后外传：The Winter Baron","anchor":"chapter-0182","paragraphs":["The old route ended at a shallow ravine, where successive storms had cut a gaping trench. Arlo's sled had stopped on the near side, its autonomous carrier blocked against a stone. He stood safely beside it, but Celia had followed his shout and slipped below the path. Her glove moved against the snow. Mara's heart gave one brutal thump, then settled into the work before her. Their co-guide radioed for warm layers, a stretcher, and so forth.","Cassian called the guide on the radio. Mara knelt where the ground had been checked, staying back from the broken edge. She would do her utmost to keep Celia talking without asking her to move. The photographer answered clearly. Her wrist hurt, and she could not climb the slick bank alone. The guide instructed them to wait for his equipment; the marked shelter was less than five minutes away.","Arlo wanted to descend immediately. His paternal need to fix everything had become a danger. \"I am out to rescue my wife,\" he snapped.","The well-known human rights photographer had trusted her husband's judgment. He was to blame for the detour, and the realization did not make him conventionally heroic. Fear threatened to impel him forward recklessly. Cassian gave a forthright refusal. 'You would obstruct the guide.' They were confronted with enough danger without another person going over the edge.","\"Then commit to the guide's plan,\" Mara said. \"Not your pride.\"","A case in point, Celia called from below: he was arguing with the person helping her. The line was apt enough to stop him. He looked at the trench, at his own footprints, and began to grieve the easy confidence of ten minutes earlier. His mistake could not be counteracted by a grand gesture. He moved back and helped keep other guests off the weakened path.","The guide arrived with a second trained worker. They checked the approach before setting their equipment. Mara and Cassian carried what they were asked to carry and did not improvise a spectacular descent. Their task was to keep the rest of the group warm and together. The short-term urge to rush could aggravate a bad situation. A decent rescue depended on several ordinary people accepting limited jobs.","Linn had stopped filming. She was unaware that her camera still streamed an empty patch of sky until Cassian pointed it out. She ended the stream instantly. Her first instinct had been to capitalize on a dramatic scene; her second was to protect a person. Earned media would mean nothing if Celia's face became entertainment at the expense of her privacy.","Arlo's bone-showing hands trembled after he removed his gloves. Mara gave them back. \"You can be affected by cold even while being furious,\" she said. Arlo exclaimed once, then stopped trying to argue aggressively.","\"As we all know,\" he muttered.","Arlo could not foresee how quickly the weather would change. The clouds might undermine the timetable, but his companions could still end up with a safe return. Linn put her notes in chronological order and began to weep quietly. She had been unaware how badly she wanted Celia to like her. The feeling was intrinsically human, and she would not betray it by turning it into footage.","\"You keep saying that.\"","\"A dismal habit.\"","His familiar wit returned only for a moment. The retired anti-patent campaigner, who could encompass a whole workshop with his noisy welcome, looked suddenly very old. Celia had always called his hospitality assimilative. Now he let the newcomers organize his own household. A feudal king accepting help from peasants, he said bitterly. Mara refused to laugh at the attempt to devalue himself. \"A husband waiting for his wife,\" she answered.","The guide's team brought Celia up slowly. Mara stayed with the other guests until they were clear. Cassian wrapped the photographer in a spare layer and helped her to the marked hut. Her injury would be assessed properly below. The visible scratch was skin-deep, but nobody assumed that settled the painful wrist. She wept once, angrily, and then asked Arlo to stop apologizing until he could breathe.","Inside the hut, a faded presidential portrait hung beside the old photograph of the White House. Celia had donated both years ago. Her brother appeared in a third picture, laughing beside a snowman. She looked at it for a long time. \"He'd have called me an idiot,\" she said.","\"Would he?\" Mara asked.","\"Lovingly.\"","Her grief had survived all over the world; it could accompany her home too. She did not need the race to cure it. Arlo sat beside her, insatiable cleverness reduced to the plain wish that she would be all right. His liberal education had given him words for every mechanism. None could improve on keeping her gloved hand in his.","The guide considered the group as a whole. They would return by the lower marked track after the injured guest was transported. No one would memorize an improvised shortcut. The route's markers denoted real turns, not suggestions to be overruled by enthusiasm. The inclement weather was holding off, but a looming cloud made delay unwise. Cassian helped the guide check names alphabetically against the starting list.","Linn began to summarize the morning, then stopped. Her camera could illustrate a forest or a sled without recording the worst minute of Celia's day. She would reassess the feature after speaking to the couple. Arlo's gadgets had been characterized glowingly; the feature needed to admit the human limits around them. That did not require a ruthless turn from praise to ridicule.","\"Will this amount to a ruined holiday?\" Celia asked Mara.","\"A delayed lunch.\"","She laughed weakly. \"An eternal optimist.\"","\"Only about food.\"","Arlo offered a reward in moola to anybody who could get them home quickly. The guide refused payment for a reckless shortcut. Fair play meant accepting the same safe plan as everyone else. No hi-tech carrier would be allowed to predetermine it. The cumulative effect of three clear refusals finally made the inventor nod.","Mara caught Cassian's hand as they prepared to leave. He had kept pace with every change without making a performance of courage. The pleasure she took in him was becoming a familiar, astonishing ache. He kissed her forehead, then helped carry the bags. The race was officially over; the day still had people to bring home."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1056,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0182\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：The Winter Baron</div><h1>The Husband at the Edge</h1><p id=\"chapter-0182-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5694\" data-words=\"5694\">old</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7174\" data-words=\"7174\">route</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2793\" data-words=\"2793\">ended</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-593\" data-words=\"593\">at</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7459\" data-words=\"7459\">shallow</a> ravine, <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9199\" data-words=\"9199\">where</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8098\" data-words=\"8098\">successive</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7994\" data-words=\"7994\">storms</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3834\" data-words=\"3834\">had</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2078\" data-words=\"2078\">cut</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3511\" data-words=\"3511\">gaping</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8665\" data-words=\"8665\">trench</a>.
